> Council to adopt amendments to the FY2025/2026 Schedule of Fees and Charges including updates to Ranger Services, Wembley Golf Course, Quarry Amphitheatre, Building Services, Works fees and charges, and Development Assessment Panel Application fees, with implementation from 1 August 2025 following advertising.

#### M2 · #2 — Grantham Street Multi-Intersection Project – Evaluation Report · *—*
Moved by **—** · Seconded by **—**

> Post-implementation evaluation of the Grantham Street Multi-Intersection Project at the intersections of Jersey and Pangbourne Streets with Grantham Street, including findings on road safety, traffic redistribution, and recommendations for additional infrastructure and placemaking initiatives.

**Motion text:**
> That Council: 1. NOTES the findings in the officers' report, and attached technical studies, community surveys and safety review; 2. SUPPORTS the following actions to be completed in the 2025/2026 financial year which are projected to enhance safety, amenity, and long-term project outcomes: (a) Collaboration with local businesses and Wembley Primary School to determine placemaking initiatives at the Grantham Street Local Centre, including opportunities for improved Alfresco Dining, landscaping and improved pedestrian amenity; (b) Installation of improved signage to clarify turn restrictions and reinforce the revised road layout at the intersection of Grantham and Jersey Streets; (c) Installation of traffic calming measures in Griffiths Lane and the adjoining car park to discourage cut-through traffic and encourage low vehicle speeds; (d) Kerb radii adjustments at the intersections of Herdsman Parade with Simper Street and Pangbourne Street to reduce entry speeds into local roads and improve pedestrian safety; (e) The replacement of the painted median with a raised solid median on Grantham Street between Jersey Street and Griffiths Lane and relocate u-turn sign, to discourage non-compliant U-turns and support streetscape and tree planting enhancements; 3. NOTES items 1(a), 1(b) and 1(c) will be funded through existing operational budgets. 4. APPROVES BY ABSOLUTE MAJORITY an amendment to the 2025/26 Annual Budget by decreasing $60,000 from 'S1308 – Investigate Wembley Road Network Improvements' to use for the Grantham Street Multi-Intersection Project. 5. APPROVES BY ABSOLUTE MAJORITY an amendment to the 2025/26 Annual Budget to create a new capital cost code, "Grantham Street Multi-Intersection Project" for $60,000. 6. NOTES that a project to address items 1(e) will be included for consideration in the draft 2026/2027 Annual Budget.

**Source quote(s):**
> ADMINISTRATION RECOMMENDATION: That Council: 1. NOTES the findings in the officers' report, and attached technical studies, community surveys and safety review; 2. SUPPORTS the following actions to be completed in the 2025/2026 financial year which are projected to enhance safety, amenity, and long-term project outcomes: *(unverified)*
> 4. APPROVES BY ABSOLUTE MAJORITY an amendment to the 2025/26 Annual Budget by decreasing $60,000 from 'S1308 – Investigate Wembley Road Network Improvements' to use for the Grantham Street Multi-Intersection Project. 5. APPROVES BY ABSOLUTE MAJORITY an amendment to the 2025/26 Annual Budget to create a new capital cost code, "Grantham Street Multi-Intersection Project" for $60,000. *(unverified)*

*Tags: infrastructure,road safety,traffic management,placemaking,budget*

#### M3 · 11.9 — Jersey Grantham Blackspot Program · *CARRIED*
Moved by **—** · Seconded by **—** · For: 9 · Against: 0

> Road safety treatment options for the intersection of Jersey and Grantham Streets, Wembley. Decision on multi-intersection treatment implementation.

**Motion text:**
> That Council: - 1. RECEIVES the Community Forums Report as shown in Attachment 1; 2. NOTES the outcome of the Special Electors Meeting in relation to Jersey Grantham Street intersection; 3. NOTES the petition received from Tania Nicolo from Monsterella Pizza requesting a permanent treatment that does not include traffic lights; 4. NOTES the petition received from Steve Godden (Simper Street) that supports the installation of traffic lights at the intersection; 5. NOTES the petitions received of 109 signatures received from Bethany Brajkovich of McKenzie Street Wembley to request that the Town of Cambridge works with Main Roads Department to install lights at the intersection of Jersey Street and Grantham Street to prevent more accidents at this intersection; 6. NOTES the petitions received of 43 signatures received from Evita Soldo of Holland Street Wembley requesting a centralised, logical, controlled place for traffic to cross east-west across Grantham Street, between Selby St and Harborne St; 7. INSTRUCTS the CEO to undertake improvements to sightlines at the intersections of Grantham Street between Harborne and Selby Street; 8. SUPPORTS a. progression of the multi-intersection treatment to detailed design and construction in the 2023/2024 Financial year; b. the continuation of the multi-intersection treatment trail at Jersey and Pangbourne at Grantham Streets until construction commences; c. an amount of $30,000 to be included for consideration in the 2024/2025 Draft Budget for a detailed traffic study to evaluate the performance of the Blackspot intersection treatment; 9. APPROVES an additional allocation of $120,000 from the Area Improvement Reserve to create a new project to investigate methods for lowering the speed zones, within the local area bounded by Selby Street, Herdsman Parade, Cambridge Street and Harborne Street, and to include the investigation of the installation of traffic signals; 10. INVESTIGATES other traffic treatments such as a roundabout and an integrated traffic management study from Southport St to Selby St; and 11. NOTES the development of a Movement Network Strategy to incorporate the long-term vision for the Town.

**Source quote(s):**
> It is recommended that the Town utilises the Blackspot Program funding to install multi-intersection treatment in the 2023/2024 financial year. This will provide an immediate response to the road safety issues at the intersection of Jersey and Grantham Streets. *(unverified)*
> The multi-intersection treatment reduces the likelihood and severity of crashes of right-angle crashes, while respecting the dominant and desired southerly vehicle movements through the Town's Road network. *(unverified)*

*Tags: infrastructure,road safety,traffic management,planning,budget*

Instructs the CEO to immediately on 29 May 2024 refund the money received from the proponent of the child care development on 130 and 132 Brookdale Street, Floreat, for works to close the median strip near that property and south of the Brookdale Street and Oceanic Drive intersection; and 2. Instructs the CEO to not obtain any legal advice on this matter." 4. ### Public Questions (9)
#### Public Question 1
- **Questioner Name:** Elson Goh
- **Question Summary:** Does the Council's traffic data indicate a speeding issue on Jersey North before the Jersey Grantham Street modifications?
- **Response Summary:** Acting Director Infrastructure and Works responded that review of data suggests there was no speeding issue at the 85th percentile speeds, which are well below the 50K limit.

**Source quote(s):**
> Elson Goh, Wembley
> Question 1
> Good evening everyone, my name is Elson from Simper St in Wembley. First question is does the Council's traffic data indicate a speeding issue on Jersey North before the Jersey Grantham Street modifications? *(unverified)*
> Response from Acting Director Infrastructure and Works
> Thank you for your question, so with that one, no there's been a review of the data that suggests there was no speeding issue at the 85th percentile speeds, which are well below the 50K limit. *(unverified)*

<!-- AUDIT: [Y/N/PARTIAL] — notes: -->

- **Questioner Name:** Elson Goh
- **Question Summary:** Why did the Council find it necessary to install six speed bumps, double the usual amount if there wasn't a speeding problem?
- **Response Summary:** Acting Director Infrastructure and Works took the question on notice.

**Source quote(s):**
> Question 2
> Thank you. Second question, why did the Council find it fit and find it necessary to install six speed bumps, double the usual amount if there wasn't a speeding problem? *(unverified)*

#### Public Question 3
- **Questioner Name:** Elson Goh
- **Question Summary:** Since Simper Street is now handling the majority of the suburbs traffic far more than Jersey North ever did, could we request similar modifications to our street to improve safety?
- **Response Summary:** Acting Director Infrastructure and Works advised that a review of that treatment is currently being undertaken early in the new year to determine the best way forward with any modifications that may or may not be required.

**Source quote(s):**
> Follow on comment
> Ok cause Jersey North has got six and Simper Street has got only three.  Third question, since Simper Street is now handling the majority of the suburbs traffic far more than Jersey North ever did, could we request similar modifications to our street to improve safety? *(unverified)*
> Response from Acting Director Infrastructure and Works
> As mentioned previously, Elson we're currently undertaking a review of that treatment which will occur early in the new year and as a result of that, we'll be able to determine the best way forward with any modifications that may or may not be required. *(unverified)*

#### M18 · 13.3.6 — Application for Development Approval - Ocean Village Precinct · *—*
Moved by **—** · Seconded by **—**

> Mixed-use development application at Ocean Village Shopping Centre site proposing towers of 22, 10, and 3 storeys with 247 multiple dwellings, 6,480m² commercial floor space, and 718 car bays. Application referred by Department of Planning, Lands and Heritage under Part 17 Significant Development Pathway.

**Motion text:**
> That Council: 1. RECOMMENDS that the Western Australian Planning Commission refuses the application for development approval for a proposed mixed-use development at the Ocean Village Shopping Centre site for the following reasons: a) The proposal involves a material and meaningful departure from both the Local Planning Strategy and Council's adopted position on the development of the site, by way of building height, and the departure would represent an unacceptable built form outcome that is incompatible with the surrounding (current and anticipated future) low-density residential context. b) The proposal involves a material and meaningful departure from State Planning Policies 4.2 and 7.3, by way of building height, and the departure would represent an unacceptable built form outcome that is incompatible with the surrounding (current and anticipated future) low-density residential context. c) The applicant has failed to demonstrate how the proposal appropriately meets Design Principle 3 contained within State Planning Policy 7.0. 2. AUTHORISES the CEO, in the event that the WAPC intends on approving the application, to provide a series of recommended conditions to the WAPC for inclusion in such an approval.

**Planning:** SDAU-066-23 · Ocean Village Shopping Centre site, City Beach; Lot 243 (No. 3) Kilpa Court, City Beach; Lot 245 (No. 5) Kilpa Court, City Beach; Part of Lot 241 and Part of Kilpa Court · status: *PENDING*
  Mixed-use development comprising two-storey podium with two basement levels, two towers with maximum heights of 20 storeys (northern) and 8 storeys (southern), and separate 3-storey building. Includes

**Source quote(s):**

### Motions (3)

#### M1 · #1 — Annual Report 2023/24 and Annual Meeting of Electors · *—*
Moved by **—** · Seconded by **—**

> Acceptance of the Annual Report and setting of the date for the Annual Meeting of Electors in accordance with Section 5.27 of the Local Government Act 1995

**Source quote(s):**
> Section 5.27 of the Act requires that a general meeting of electors of a district is to be held once every financial year and that the general meeting is held on a day selected by the local government, not more than 56 days after the local government accepts the Annual Report. *(unverified)*
> At least 14 days local public notice is required of the date, time, place and purpose of the Annual Meeting of Electors. The recommended date for the meeting of electors is based on meeting the above legislative requirements talking into account administrative considerations and considering that many people take holidays from the middle of December to late in January. *(unverified)*

*Tags: governance,procedural,annual reporting*

#### M3 · 7.1.1 — Annual Report for the 2023/24 Financial Year · *—*
Moved by **—** · Seconded by **—**

> Council to approve and adopt the Town of Cambridge Annual Report for 2023/24, incorporating the Annual Financial Statements and Auditor's Report, and to hold the Annual General Meeting of Electors on 11 February 2025.

**Motion text:**
> That Council: 1. APPROVES BY ABSOLUTE MAJORITY, pursuant to Section 5.54 of the Local Government Act 1995, the Town of Cambridge 2023-2024 Annual Report incorporating the Annual Financial Statements and the Auditor's Report 2023-2024, as shown in Attachment 1; and 2. Submits the Annual Report 2023-2024 for adoption to the Annual General Meeting of Electors, subject to the Chief Executive Officer making any minor amendments to the text as necessary; and (a) RESOLVES to hold the Annual General Meeting of Electors of the Town of Cambridge on Tuesday 11 February 2025, at the Town's Administration Centre, 1 Bold Park Drive, Floreat, commencing at 6pm. (b) APPROVES the notice of the Annual General Meeting of Electors to be advertised in accordance with the provisions of Section 5.29 of the Local Government Act 1995 (c) CONSIDERS, pursuant to Section 5.33 of the Local Government Act 1995, any motions made at the Annual General Meeting of Electors, which will be presented to the next practical Ordinary Council meeting.

**Source quote(s):**
> APPROVES BY ABSOLUTE MAJORITY, pursuant to Section 5.54 of the Local Government Act 1995, the Town of Cambridge 2023-2024 Annual Report incorporating the Annual Financial Statements and the Auditor's Report 2023-2024, as shown in Attachment 1 *(unverified)*
> Submits the Annual Report 2023-2024 for adoption to the Annual General Meeting of Electors, subject to the Chief Executive Officer making any minor amendments to the text as necessary *(unverified)*
